Let us focus on the region where the width-to-mass ratios are relatively small (i.e.
\leq 10\%
), a condition always satisfied in our study. We see that the mass ratio
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{T}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}
plays a crucial role in determining the validity (or invalidity) of the NWA at both LO and NLO levels. For the low-mass case,
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{T}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}=1/2
, we find that the LO cross sections in NWA
1
and NWA
2
are approximately equal to CM scheme results, with a relative difference of up to
4\%
for most of the considered cases, see table
8
and the solid curves in the left panels. Thus, we can write:
\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle\text{LO}}^{\text{NWA}_{1}}\approx\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle\text{LO}}^{\text{NWA}_{2}}\approx\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle\text{LO}}^{\text{CM}}
, which is not always correct for the high-mass case.

However, at NLO, the relative difference between NWA
1
and CM is larger than for NWA
2
, where it can exceeds the
15\%
. Consequently, one can take with caution
\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle\text{NLO}}^{\text{NWA}_{2}}\approx\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle\text{NLO}}^{\text{CM}}
for low-mass case, since the discrepancy is about
3\%
in
{\bf T^{\scriptscriptstyle\{3\}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\{Z,Z^{\prime}\}}}
and
7\%
in
{\bf T^{\scriptscriptstyle\{3\}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\{Z,H,Z^{\prime}\}}}
, see tables
6
and
7
.
The situation changes significantly for the high-mass case,
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{T}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}=5/6
(see the right panels of figure
14
). Here, the relative differences are much larger than in the previous case, they can exceed the
20\%
for both LO or NLO, which is not negligible, see table
9
. This indicates that off-shell effects cannot be ignored in this regime, which render the NWA unreliable. Instead, one has to employ the CM scheme.

The important conclusion that we take from the previous discussion is that if the mass is close to the decay threshold of the
{Z^{\prime}}
into
Tt
, the NWA become invalid and the CM scheme which is, available at LO and NLO for our model, must be used. Conversely, if the mass is far from the decay threshold, one may use NWA
2
at NLO but with caution, see appendix
C
for more details.

For completeness, we provide the differential cross section in terms of the invariant mass of the leading jets in figure
15
. We observe that the differential distributions are globally similar, with the only difference lying in the height of the peaks. See, the discussion at the end of the previous subsection.

Figure 15:
LO and NLO invariant mass differential distribution, for the middle-mass case and
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}=2.5
TeV, in DD scenario (with
p_{\scriptscriptstyle{Tj}}>20
,
|\eta_{\scriptscriptstyle{j}}|<5
and
\Delta R_{\scriptscriptstyle{jj}}>0.4
).

To conclude this section, we emphasize that this model stands out as a robust candidate for constraining the masses of the
W^{\prime}
and
{Z^{\prime}}
boson decaying into VLQ. It offers a significant advantage over other models by requiring fewer free parameters (only two in DD scenario) and facilitates the NLO predictions, which provide more precise results, thanks to the
UFO
NLO model automatically generated by
FeynRules/NLOCT/FeynArts
(which perform the automatic generation of all UV counter-terms and rational terms).

4
Summary and prospects

In the present paper, we have provided an extension of the SM including a singlet VLQ (
T
or
B
), the heavy gauge bosons
W^{\prime},Z^{\prime}
and the scalar
H^{\prime}
, so that their mixing with SM particles is model-independent. VLQs are assumed to mix with SM particles via the Yukawa interaction, whereas we assumed that they mix only with 3rd generation quarks in the phenomenological part of this work. The main idea behind the parameterization of the couplings, in our approach, is to express them in terms of branching ratios of the new heavy particles and the kinematic functions related to their partial decays. In order to be as general as possible in the derivation of the couplings, we considered two mass hierarchy configurations:
(i)
the VLQ is heavier than the supplementary bosons and
(ii)
the extra bosons are heavier than the VLQ. To remain as model-independent as possible, we did not include the 3- and 4-vertex of the heavy gauge bosons and scalars, nor the weak and electromagnetic parts in the covariant derivative associated with the VLQ.

The model couplings are theoretically constrained from perturbative unitarity procedure by requiring that the highest eigenvalues of the partial-wave amplitudes be less than the unity. Restrictive bounds on the parameters normalizing the couplings have been set for VLQ and heavy bosons masses explorable at the LHC, showing that the perturbative treatment is valid in our phenomenological study. This model is renormalized at one-loop in QCD using the automatic tools
FeynRules/NLCT/FeynArts
, where 3 versions of the model in
UFO
format for the 4-, 5- and 6- flavor-schemes, available at the NLO and the complex mass scheme, are generated. This model is validated by comparing its predictions with those of other existing models, including VLQs or heavy bosons, and with the SM predictions for specific processes and configurations.

In the phenomenological part of this work, we highlighted the case where the masses of the bosons
W^{\prime}
and
Z^{\prime}
are larger than the mass of
T
(
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{T}}=1/2,2/3,3/4\,m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
and
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{T}}=1/2,2/3,5/6\,m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}
). This means that these bosons can decay into a VLQ and a 3rd generation quark, where the VLQ can decay only to SM particles. In the 4FS, we studied the production of the gauge bosons
{W^{\prime}}
and
{Z^{\prime}}
, in proton-proton collision, which then decay to top partner
T
at center-of-mass energy
\sqrt{s}=13
TeV, where several benchmark scenarios were explored. We find that for LHC mass range, the perturbative treatment is always available in this model. We calculated the cross sections at LO and NLO in QCD by adopting 3 approaches: the complex-mass scheme (full calculation), the narrow-width approximation (where
\mbox{${W^{\prime}}$}/\mbox{${Z^{\prime}}$}
and
T
are treated as on-shell states), and a mixture of both (where only the
T
is considered as resonant state).

We find that, the LO predictions from CM scheme, NWA
1
and NWA
2
are nearly identical, with
acceptable relative differences in both cases of the production of
{W^{\prime}}
or
{Z^{\prime}}
, if their masses are far from their decay thresholds to VLQ. However, the NLO predictions for NWA
1
and NWA
2
are
not as close to CM scheme predictions in certain configurations.
We demonstrated that the relative differences between CM and NWA
2
predictions are smaller compared to those between CM and NWA
1
. However, we found that the discrepancy between CM scheme and NWA
2
predictions may be very important (and large sometimes), when the mass of the gauge bosons is close to the decay threshold, especially in the case of
{Z^{\prime}}
production. Thus, the CM scheme remains the best option that can be used to compute the NLO cross sections, fortunately this is available in our model.

We also demonstrated that our model is not excluded by
run II
results and it represents a promising model that can be used for the hunting of VLQs and heavy gauge bosons. We have shown that for
\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle T}$}=0.1
, the lower
{W^{\prime}}
mass limit is set between
2.8-3.8
TeV for
\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
ranging between 0.3 and 0.8 in DD scenario.

This model predicts the existence of a scalar particle
{H^{\prime}}
capable to interact simultaneously with both SM particles and VLQs. This type of particle plays a major role in BSM physics. In this work, we did not include the phenomenological study of such particle at the LHC due to the length of this article. We therefore postpone this part of the work to a separate publication.

As we mentioned earlier, we generated a
UFO
model in the 6FS. This should, in principle, be more reliable than the 5FS and 4FS at very high energies (at the very large future colliders) and it would also lead to new production mechanisms
[
28
]
. Exploring the physics of VLQs and the extra-heavy gauge bosons and scalars in this scheme would be a promising route for investigation.

Complete the model by including the missing peaces such as interaction (and self-interaction) between the different gauge bosons and scalars, taking into account the weak and electromagnetic components in the covariant derivative associated with VLQs …, etc. This will restore partial-wave unitarity for processes involving real heavy gauge bosons in the initial or final states, like processes discussed in appendix
B
, and would be a step towards one-loop electroweak renormalization of the model. We aim to achieve this in the near future.

Appendix A
More detail on the model independant parametrisation

In this appendix, we derive the model independent parameterization of the mixing of a singlet vector like-quark (
Q
) to ordinary quarks via the SM bosons (
W
,
Z
and
H
), two new heavy vector-bosons (
{W^{\prime}}
,
{Z^{\prime}}
) and a new heavy scalar (
{H^{\prime}}
).
We recall that only one VLQ of type top (
Q\equiv T
) or bottom (
Q\equiv B
) which is present in this model and not both, and it is assumed to mix only with one quark generation.
To be more general, we consider all possible mass hierarchies between the VLQs and the extra heavy bosons, on which our parameterization depends
20
20

20

Based on ATLAS and CMS publictions, it is more experimentally realistic to assume that
Q
are lighter than
W^{\prime},Z^{\prime}
and
H^{\prime}
.

A.1
Case 1:
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}>m_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}+m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}

In this case, the vector-like top quark
Q
is the heaviest particle in the model, so it can decay to an ordinary quark and one of the bosons
B^{\prime\prime}=W,\mbox{${W^{\prime}}$},Z,\mbox{${Z^{\prime}}$},H,\mbox{${H^{\prime}}$}
according to the following decay modes:

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\rightarrow qH,

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\rightarrow qZ,

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\rightarrow q^{\prime}W

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\rightarrow q\mbox{${H^{\prime}}$},

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\rightarrow q\mbox{${Z^{\prime}}$},

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\rightarrow q^{\prime}\mbox{${W^{\prime}}$}

(25)

where
q
and
q^{\prime}
denote, respectively,
up
and
dwon
-type quarks of the same generation for
Q\equiv T
, and
down
and
up
-type for
Q\equiv B
.
Assuming that only left or right handed coupling chirality (and not both) which contributes, then the partial width for each channel can be written as:

\displaystyle\Gamma(Q\rightarrow\mbox{${B^{\prime\prime}}$}q)

\displaystyle=\left(\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}$}\right)^{2}\frac{g^{2}m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{3}}{64\pi m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}^{2}}\,\,\,\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}}}(m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}})

(26)

where
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}}}
,
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}
and
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}
stand, respectively, for the masses of the boson
{B^{\prime\prime}}
, the VLQ
Q
and the SM quark
q

21
21

21

Typically,
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}
is neglected, except when mixing with the 3
rd
generation, where it may take the value of
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{t}}
or
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{b}}
(in 4FS).
. The parameters
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}
are related to the parameters
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}
of the Lagarngian
\mbox{${\cal L}$}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
, cf. eq. (
2
), by:

\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}

\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}\,\,\,\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}

\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}\,\,\,\frac{v}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}}

\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}

\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}\,\,\,\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}

\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}\,\,\,\frac{v}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}}

(27)

and
\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}}}
are the kinematic functions, they are given by:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{V}}(m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{V}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}})

\displaystyle=\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\biggl(1,\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{V}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{2}},\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{2}}\biggr)\biggl[\biggl(1-\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{2}}\biggr)^{2}+\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{V}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{2}}-2\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{V}}^{4}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{4}}+\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}^{2}m_{\scriptscriptstyle{V}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{4}}\biggr]/\gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{V}}

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{S}}(m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{S}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}})

\displaystyle=\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}}\biggl(1,\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{S}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{2}},\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{2}}\biggr)\biggl[1+\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{2}}-\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{S}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{2}}\biggr]/2

(28)

where
V
and
S
stand for vector and scalar bosons,
\gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}=\gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}=1
,
\gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}=\gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}=2
and
\lambda(x,y,z)=x^{2}+y^{2}+z^{2}-2xy-2xz-2yz
.

To express the partial decay width of
Q
into scalars as in the general formula given in eq. (
26
), one has to replace the SM Higgs
vev
by
v=2m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}/g
. We note that the
vev
of the new scalar (
v^{\prime}
) is hidden in the parameter
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}
, i.e.
\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=(v/v^{\prime})\mbox{$\tilde{\tilde{\kappa}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}
, where
\tilde{\tilde{\kappa}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}
is a free parameter normalizing the coupling
H^{\prime}-Q-q
as:
\mbox{$\tilde{\tilde{\kappa}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}\,m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}/v^{\prime}
. We employ this trick to remain as independent as possible of the BSM mode,l which might contains other extra neutral scalars.

For mixing with one quark generation, the branching ratio of this decay
Q\rightarrow\mbox{${B^{\prime\prime}}$}q
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(Q\rightarrow B^{\prime\prime}q)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}$})^{2}\,\,\,\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}}}(m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}})}{\underset{\begin{subarray}{l}\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}=W,W^{\prime},}\\
\scriptscriptstyle{Z,Z^{\prime},H,H^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}{\sum}(\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}$})^{2}\,\,\,\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}(m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}})}\equiv\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}}}

(29)

We observe that in the asymptotic limit (
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}\rightarrow\infty
) and if all
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}
are equal, the VLQ decays 50% to charged gauge bosons (25% to
W
and 25% to
{W^{\prime}}
) and 25% to neutral gauge bosons (12.5% to
Z
and 12.5% to
{Z^{\prime}}
) and 25% to scalars (12.5% to
H
and 12.5% to
{H^{\prime}}
), which is in agreement with Goldstone equivalence theorem
22
22

22

The Goldstone equivalence theorem states that at high energies, the amplitude of the emission or absorbtion of longitudinally polarized massive gauge boson is equal to the amplitude of the emission or absorbtion of the corresponding Goldstone boson. In our case, this translates to:
\xi_{W}=2\,\xi_{Z}=2\,\xi_{H}
and
\xi_{W^{\prime}}=2\,\xi_{Z^{\prime}}=2\,\xi_{H^{\prime}}
for
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}\rightarrow\infty
.
, see sub.figure
16a
. To show this, we set the fraction of the masses on the functions
\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}}}
to zero, i.e.
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}^{2}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{2}
,
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}^{2}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{2}\rightarrow 0
which is justified for very large
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}
(
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}\gg m_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}
and
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}\gg m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}
), we get

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}|_{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}\gg}}

\displaystyle\approx\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}^{0}=1

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}|_{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}\gg}}

\displaystyle\approx\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}^{0}=1

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{H}}|_{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}\gg}}

\displaystyle\approx\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{H}}^{0}=1/2

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}|_{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}\gg}}

\displaystyle\approx\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}^{0}=1/2

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}|_{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}\gg}}

\displaystyle\approx\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}^{0}=1/2

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{H^{\prime}}}|_{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}\gg}}

\displaystyle\approx\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{H^{\prime}}}^{0}=1/2

(30)

It is clear, in this limit, that
\xi_{W,W^{\prime}}=2\,\xi_{Z,Z^{\prime}}=2\,\xi_{H,H^{\prime}}=1/4
. We notice that the latter result is not true if
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}
are not equal even for very large
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}
. For example, if we take
\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=1
and
\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
,
\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}
and
\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=v/v^{\prime}
, and if we assume that
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}=m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}=v/v^{\prime}
, we find that
\xi_{W}=2\,\xi_{Z}=2\,\xi_{H}
and
\xi_{W^{\prime}}=2\,\xi_{Z^{\prime}}=2\,\xi_{H^{\prime}}
, where

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2(1+v^{2}/v^{\prime\,2})}

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{v^{2}/v^{\prime\,2}}{2(1+v^{2}/v^{\prime\,2})}

(31)

then for
v^{\prime}=2\,v
, we get
\xi_{W}=2\,\xi_{Z}=2\,\xi_{H}=40\%
and
\xi_{W^{\prime}}=2\,\xi_{Z^{\prime}}=2\,\xi_{H^{\prime}}=10\%
, see sub.figureure
16b
.

(a)

(b)

Figure 16:
Variation of
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle B^{\prime\prime}}
in term of
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}
(
Q\equiv T
) for
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime},Z^{\prime},H^{\prime}}}=1000\,\text{GeV}
and
\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}$}=1
(left) and for
\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=1
and
\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
,
\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}
and
\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=v/v^{\prime}
(right).

In the left panel of Figure. (
16
), we show the evolution of the branching ratios of
Q
as a function of
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}
for
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime},Z^{\prime},H^{\prime}}}=1
TeV and equal
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}
. We observe that when
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}
is of the same order as
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime},Z^{\prime},H^{\prime}}}
, the branching ratios
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W,Z,H}}
dominate over
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime},Z^{\prime},H^{\prime}}}
. However, they become equal for large
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}
as expected (i.e.
\xi_{W}=\xi_{W^{\prime}}
,
\xi_{Z}=\xi_{Z^{\prime}}
and
\xi_{H}=\xi_{H^{\prime}}
). In the right panel, we show the variation of the branching ratio for
\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=1
with
\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
,
\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}
and
\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$}=v/v^{\prime}
. Here, we assume
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}=m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}=v/v^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}=2\,v
. For large
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}
, we find
\xi_{W}=2\,\xi_{Z}=2\,\xi_{H}\approx 40\%
and
\xi_{W^{\prime}}=2\,\xi_{Z^{\prime}}=2\,\xi_{H^{\prime}}\approx 10\%
, which is consistent with our expectations.

The key motivation behind this parameterization is to treat the branching ratios as free parameters rather than as functions of particle masses and couplings, as we done previousely, cf. Figure. (
16
). So, the idea is to express the parameters
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}
in terms of
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}}}
and the kinematic functions
\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}}}
. To achieve this, we introduce a new free parameter
\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
, defined such that
\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}^{2}=\sum_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}(\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}$})^{2}\,\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}
. This modifies eq. (
29
) as follows:

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}$})^{2}\,\,\,\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}}}(m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}})}{\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}^{2}}

(32)

Then, the parameters
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}
can be easily expressed as:

\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}}}}}

with

\displaystyle\underset{\begin{subarray}{l}\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}=W,W^{\prime},}\\
\scriptscriptstyle{Z,Z^{\prime},H,H^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}{\sum}\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime\prime}}}

\displaystyle=1

(33)

The original parameters
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}
of the Lagrangian (
2
) are then given by:

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}}}

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}}

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}}{v}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{H}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{H}}}}

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}}}

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}}

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}}{v}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{H^{\prime}}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{H^{\prime}}}}}

(34)

where
\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
is treated as free parameter which can be determined experimentally.

As a pointed out in refs.
[
24
,
25
]
, for singlet VLQ only the left-handed mixing angle is the dominant (the right-handed one is suppressed by a factor of
m_{q}/m_{T}
). So, we choose to set all the right-handed coupling to zero except those associated to the scalar sector in the case of
Q\equiv T
which mixes only with 3
rd
quark generation. In this case, we keep both coupling chiralities since the smaller one is proportional to the top quark mass (
m_{t}/v
) which is not negligible especially for relatively small
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{T}}
. So, the Lagrangian (
2
) describing the interactions of
Q
with the ordinary quarks via the six bosons is expressed in terms of the 10 independent parameters:
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}},\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}},\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}},\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}},\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{H}}
,
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{H^{\prime}}}
and
\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
in the general benchmark scenario, denoted by
{\bf Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle\{3\}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\{W,W^{\prime},Z,Z^{\prime},H,H^{\prime}\}}
, where all the new hypotetical particles are involved in the model.

At first glance, one might think that the couplings of the new heavy bosons with VLQ, cf. eq. (
34
), become very large near
Q
decay thresholds (and diverge at
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}=m_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}+m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}
, for
B^{\prime}=W^{\prime},Z^{\prime},H^{\prime}
) because of the kinematic functions
\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}
, which become very small (and vanishes at
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}=m_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}+m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}
). Actually, this is not true since in this region also the branching ratios
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}
becomes very small (and vanishes at
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}=m_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}+m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}
), see the left panel in figure
16
. In practice, the masses of VLQs and the hypotetical heavy bosons are most commonly different, so we rarely approach the dangerous region near the decay threshold. This means that the parameterization is safe for realistic phenomenological studies. However, we can slightly modify it to remain valid even in this problematic region, making it more general.

Let’s assume that
\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}\propto\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}
, then we write

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{2}\,\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}.

(35)

where
\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
is a proportionality factor which we assume that it is independent of the ratio
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}
23
23

23

In general,
\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
might be a regular function in
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}
. It can be a constant, i.e. independent of
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}
, if
\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
and
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}
are independent of such mass as we will show later on.
. Because eq. (
35
) is always satisfied whatever the masses
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
and
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}
, then it is more practical to write

\displaystyle\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}}=\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}\equiv\text{constant}.

(36)

where
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}
and
\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}
are the branching ratio and the kinematic function associated to the heavy boson
B^{\prime}
taken at the asymptotic limit
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}\rightarrow\infty
(i.e.
\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{2}=1/2
for
{\bf Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle\{3\}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\{W,W^{\prime}\}}
and
\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{2}=1/3
for
{\bf Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle\{3\}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\{W,W^{\prime},Z,H\}}
and so on). We will now express the parameters
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}
, cf. eq. (
33
), as follows:

\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}}.

(37)

which clearly show that
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R/L}}
is finite even for
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}\approx m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}
.

We will now check the validity of equations (
35
) and (
37
) in the benchmark scenario
{\bf Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle\{3\}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\{W,W^{\prime},Z,H\}}
as an example. The branching ratio
\text{Br}(Q\rightarrow W^{\prime}q)
, in this case, can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$})^{2}\,\,\,\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}(m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}},0)}{\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{2}+(\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$})^{2}\,\,\,\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}(m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}},m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}},0)}

(38)

where we neglected the masses of the ordinary quarks for simplicity and set

\displaystyle\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{2}

\displaystyle=(\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$})^{2}\,\,\,\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}+(\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$})^{2}\,\,\,\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}+(\mbox{$\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}$})^{2}\,\,\,\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{H}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}.

(39)

In eq. (
39
), we take the kinematic function in the asymptotic limit since the masses of SM particles are very small compared to
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
. So, the parameter
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}
is given by:

\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}\,\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}\,(1-\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}})}}

(40)

In the following, we will demonstrate that the value of
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}
is independent of the ratios between the masses
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
and
m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}
by evaluating it in two cases:
(i)
when
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
is much greater than
m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}
and
(ii)
when
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
and
m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}
are approximately equal
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}\approx m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}
.

•

For
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}\gg m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}
, the different branching ratios involved in this benchmark scenario are given by:

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}=\frac{1}{3}

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}

\displaystyle=\xi_{H}=\frac{1}{6}

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}+\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}+\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}+\xi_{H}

\displaystyle=1.

(41)

then

\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}}{\sqrt{2}}.

(42)

where we set
\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}\approx\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}^{0}=1
and
\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
is
\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
taken at the asymptotic limit. It can be written in term of
\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
as

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}^{2}\,\left(\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}+\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}+\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{H}}\right)\approx\frac{2}{3}\,\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}^{2}\equiv\frac{2}{3}\,\left(\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\right)^{2}.

(43)

•

Let’s assume that
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}\approx 0
, then

\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}

\displaystyle\approx\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}\,\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}}.

(44)

where
\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}\equiv\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
for
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}\approx 0
which can be expressed in term of
\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
as the following:

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\approx\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}^{2}\,\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{4}\right)=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}^{2}\equiv\left(\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}\right)^{2}.

(45)

Now, we suppose that
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}
given in eq. (
42
) is different from the one given in eq. (
44
) but both are finite, so we can write

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}}{\sqrt{2}}=\beta_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}\,\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}\,\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}}.

(46)

where
\beta_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
is a regular function on
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
.

Solving this equality, expanding the solution around
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}\rightarrow 0
and taking terms up to
\mathcal{O}\left(\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}\right)
order, we get

\displaystyle m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{2}

\displaystyle\approx m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\,\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}}{\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}}\,\beta_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}\,m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}^{2}\,\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}^{1/2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}\right)

\displaystyle\approx m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}^{2}+\frac{\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}}{\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}}\,\beta_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}\,m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}^{2}\,\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}^{1/2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}\right)

(47)

We find that the kinematic function, in this limit, is given by:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=2\,\frac{\left(\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}\right)^{2}}{\left(\tilde{\kappa}_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\right)^{2}}\,\beta_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{2}\,\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}^{3/2}\right).

\displaystyle=3\,\frac{\left(\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}\right)^{2}}{\left(\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\right)^{2}}\,\beta_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{2}\,\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}^{3/2}\right).

(48)

which confirm our assumption outlined in eq. (
35
).
Comparing eq. (
35
) and eq. (
48
), we find that:

\displaystyle\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3\,\beta_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{2}}\,\frac{\left(\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\right)^{2}}{\left(\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}\right)^{2}}.

(49)

From this equation we see that if
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}
and
\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
are the same in the two approximation, i.e.
\beta_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}=1
and
\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}=\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}\equiv\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}
, then
\alpha_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{2}=1/3
as expected.

Equation (
47
) states that: for very small
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
, the masses
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
and
m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}
are approximately equal provided that
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}
is independent of
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
. Conversely, if the the masses
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
and
m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}
are approximately the same, the branching ratio will be very small, and the parameter
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}
will retain the same value as it is in the asymptotic limit
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}\gg m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}
provided that
\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
is constant, i.e. the parameter
\tilde{\kappa}^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L/R}}
is independent of the mass of the vector like quark as expected.

While this result can be proven similarly for other benchmark scenarios, we will omit the extensive calculations to avoid an overly technical presentation. Consequently, the following equation summarizes the new form of all the model’s couplings (cf. (
34
)) in the case where
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}>m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}+m_{\scriptscriptstyle q}
for the most general benchmark scenario
{\bf Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle\{3\}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\{W,W^{\prime},Z,Z^{\prime},H,H^{\prime}\}}
.

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\,\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}},

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\,\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}},

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(S)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\,\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}}{v}\,\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle S}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}},

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\,\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle W}}\,\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}},

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\,\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle Z}}\,\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}},

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(S)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\,\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}}{v}\,\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{S}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{S}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}}.

(50)

Figure 17:
Variation of the couplings in term of the ratio
m_{\scriptscriptstyle B^{\prime}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
in the benchmark scenario
{\bf Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle\{3\}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\{W,W^{\prime},Z,Z^{\prime},H,H^{\prime}\}}
, where
\kappa_{L/R,1}^{(B^{\prime})}
and
\kappa_{L/R,2}^{(B^{\prime})}
denote, respectively, the couplings in he original and the modified parameterizations.

In figure.
17
, we show the variation of the couplings as a function of the ratio
m_{\scriptscriptstyle B^{\prime}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
, comparing the original parameterization (cf. eq.
34
) with the modified parameterization (cf. eq. (
50
)) in the benchmark scenario
{\bf Q}^{\scriptscriptstyle\{3\}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\{W,W^{\prime},Z,Z^{\prime},H,H^{\prime}\}}
. The right pannel shows the same plots as the left pannel, but we used the logarithmic scale to make the comparaison easier. These plots clearly demonstrate that both parameterizations yield approximately the same coupling values when
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
is sufficientely large compared to
m_{\scriptscriptstyle B^{\prime}}
(i.e.
m_{\scriptscriptstyle B^{\prime}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}<0.5
). However, beyond
m_{\scriptscriptstyle B^{\prime}}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}=0.5
, the diffrence starts to become significant until when the couplings become completely diffrent near the decay threshould.

The two parameterizations options given in eq. (
34
) and eq. (
50
) represent two different perspectives. Naturally, when the mass of
Q
is large compared to the heavy bosons masses, both should yield approximately the same predictions. However, beyond this regime, we cannot definitivelly say which one is better than the other. Therfore, it is recomended to consider both of them.

We note that the couplings
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}
,
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}
and
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(S)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}
are proportional to the masses
m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}
,
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}
and
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
, respectively (in both parameterization). This proportionality implies that they can become very large, which potentially could invalidate the perturbative treatment at extremely high masses. In section. (
2.3
) and appendix (
B
), we establish upper limits on the couplings using perturbative unitarity constraints to ensure that we remain within the perturbative regime.

A.2
Case 2:
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}<m_{\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\prime}}}-m_{\scriptscriptstyle{}q}

In this case, the decay of the VLQ
Q
to a SM quark and one the heavy bosons (
\mbox{${B^{\prime}}$}\equiv\mbox{${W^{\prime}}$}
,
{Z^{\prime}}
,
{H^{\prime}}
) is kinematically forbidden. So, it can decay only according to the following three modes:

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\rightarrow qH,

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\rightarrow qZ,

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\rightarrow q^{\prime}W

(51)

where
q
and
q^{\prime}
are up or down quarks.

Following the same strategy as in the previous section, we show that the parameters normalising the mixing between
Q
and SM quark via
W,Z
and
H
are given by:

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W}}}},

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z}}}},

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{T}}}{v}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{H}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{H}}}},

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(H)}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{R}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}$}\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}}{v}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{H}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{H}}}}.

(52)

with
\underset{\begin{subarray}{l}\scriptscriptstyle{B=}\scriptscriptstyle{Z,W,H}\end{subarray}}{\sum}\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{B}}=1
.

In this case, we suppose that the new heavy bosons can decay into
Q
then, following our strategy, the parameters describing the mixing between the VLQ and the bosons
{B^{\prime}}
can be expressed in terms of branching ratio of
{B^{\prime}}
into
Q
and the masses of the particles as we will show later on. We remind that the fermions of SM interact with the new bosons according to the SSM (cf. section
2
), i.e. they have the same couplings of SM the bosons up to normalization factors.

The parameter
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}

We suppose that
{W^{\prime}}
can decay into all the fermions involved in the model, then the partial decay widths of its different decay modes are given by:

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mbox{${W^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow u_{i}d_{j})=N_{c}(\mbox{$\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$})^{2}\frac{g^{2}m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}{48\pi},

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mbox{${W^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow l\nu_{l})=(\mbox{$\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$})^{2}\frac{g^{2}m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}{48\pi},

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mbox{${W^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow tb)=N_{c}(\mbox{$\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$})^{2}\frac{g^{2}m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}{48\pi}\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{tb}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}},

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mbox{${W^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow Qq^{\prime})=N_{c}(\mbox{$\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}$})^{2}\frac{g^{2}m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}{48\pi}\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}.

(53)

where we neglected the masses of the ordinary fermions (except the top quark), i.e. we set
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{f}}^{2}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}^{2}=0
(
f\neq t
) since
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}\gg m_{\scriptscriptstyle{f}}
. The kinematic functions involved in eq. (
53
) are:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{tb}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\biggr(1-\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{t}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}^{2}}\biggr)^{2}\biggl[1+\frac{1}{2}\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{t}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}^{2}}\biggr].

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}{\scriptscriptstyle q^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{1/2}\left(1,\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}^{2}},\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle q^{\prime}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}^{2}}\right)\left[1-\frac{1}{2}\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{2}+m_{\scriptscriptstyle q^{\prime}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}^{2}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{(m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}^{2}-m_{\scriptscriptstyle q^{\prime}}^{2})^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}^{4}}\right],

\displaystyle\approx\frac{1}{2}\,\left(1-\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}^{2}}\right)^{2}\,\left[2+\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}^{2}}\right].

(54)

In the asymptotic limit (i.e. for
m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}\gg m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q},m_{\scriptscriptstyle q}
), we can approximate the kinematic function to:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{t}{\scriptscriptstyle b}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}0}

\displaystyle\approx 1,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}{\scriptscriptstyle q^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}0}

\displaystyle\approx 1.

(55)

The total decay width of the
{W^{\prime}}
gauge boson is then:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}^{\text{tot}}

\displaystyle=3\,\Gamma(\mbox{${W^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow l\nu_{l})+2\,\Gamma(\mbox{${W^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow u_{i}d_{j})+\Gamma(\mbox{${W^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow tb)+\Gamma(\mbox{${W^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow Qq^{\prime})

(56)

The branching fraction of
{W^{\prime}}
to top VLQ
Q
and a ordinary quark
q^{\prime}
, in the SSM, is given by:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(\mbox{${W^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow Qq^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{(\mbox{$\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}$})^{2}\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}{(\mbox{$\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$})^{2}+2(\mbox{$\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$})^{2}+(\mbox{$\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$})^{2}\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{tb}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}+(\mbox{$\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}$})^{2}\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}\equiv\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}

(57)

Now, let us turn our discussion to the behavior of the branching ratio
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
as a function of the parameter
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}
and the mass
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
, cf. eq. (
57
). This quantity increase with the mass of
{W^{\prime}}
because the phase space of the processes becomes larger, as shown in the left panel of Figure.
18
. In the asymptotic limit
m_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}\rightarrow\infty
, which corresponds to the highest branching ratio,
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
depends solely on the parameter
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}
and is given by:
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}\sim(\mbox{$\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}$})^{2}/(4+(\mbox{$\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}$})^{2})
. In this limit, the
{W^{\prime}}
decays
5.88\%
,
20\%
and
50\%
to
Qq^{\prime}
for
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}
equals to 0.5, 1 and 2, respectively. In fact, for our phenomenological studies, we are dealing with branching ratios less than
50\%
which is more realistic scenario. Indeed, we will later demonstrate that in CSSM (i.e.
\mbox{$\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$}=\mbox{$\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$}=1
),
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
is approximately
20\%
if
W^{\prime}
decay democratically to all kind of quark flavors.

Figure 18:
Variation of the
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
in terms of
m_{W^{\prime}}
and
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}
.

The main idea of our strategy is to set the denominator of eq. (
57
) equals to
\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}$}^{2}
where the later one is treated as a free parameter
24
24

24

In the SSM, the parameter
\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}
is no longer free; it is constrained by the condition:
\mbox{$\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$}=\mbox{$\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$}
, as we will see later.
, i.e. the branching ratio
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
is expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\mbox{$\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}$})^{2}\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}{\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}$}^{2}}

(58)

Now, we can express the coupling
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}
in terms of the branching ratio, the kinematic function
\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
and the parameter
\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}
:

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}$}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}}

(59)

Similarly, the couplings of
{W^{\prime}}
to SM quarks and lepton are given by:

\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}$}\sqrt{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{u_{i}d_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}

\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}$}\sqrt{3\,\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{l_{i}l_{\nu_{i}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}

(60)

where we take
\Gamma_{tb}^{W^{{}^{\prime}}}\approx 1
(since
m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}\gg m_{\scriptscriptstyle t}
), and ‘the branching ratios must satisfy:

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\left[\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{u_{i}d_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}+\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{l_{i}l_{\nu_{i}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}\right]

\displaystyle=1.

(61)

As we have already stated in the previous section, eq. (
59
) is not valid when
m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}\approx m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
because of the threshold singularity caused by the vanishing of
\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
in this regions. In our phenomenological study, we never approach this kinematic configuration, so our parameterization remain safe (we consider
m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}=1/2,\,2/3\,3/4\,m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
, cf. section
3
). However, for completeness, we can slightly modify this parameterization such that the coupling remains well-defined even when
m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}\approx m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
.

To do so, we replace
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
and
\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
with their asymptotic values (at
m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}\rightarrow\infty
), which is possible under the following requirement:

\displaystyle\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}=\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}0}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}0}}}\equiv\text{constant}

(62)

Thus, the coupling of the
{W^{\prime}}
to
Q
, in the modified parameterization, becomes:

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}$}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}0}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}0}}}

(63)

In the left panel of figure
19
, we show the variation of the coupling
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}
in the original parameterization (cf. eq.
59
) and the modified parameterization (cf. eq.
63
) in term of the ratio
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}
for
m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}=4
TeV. The green, red and blue curves represent, respectively, the coupling for
\mbox{$\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$}=\mbox{$\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$}=2,1,0.5
. It is clear that the couplings in the two parameterizations are very close when
m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}
is sufficiently larger than
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}
especially for
\mbox{$\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$}=\mbox{$\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$}=0.5
, which implies that the two approaches are equivalent in this configurations.

Figure 19:
Variation of
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(B^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}
as a function of the ratio
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle B^{\prime}}
in the original and the modified parameterizations.

According to eq. (
60
), the parameter
\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}
is not any more free but it is constrained by the branching ratio and the SSM couplings.
From eq. (
60
), we deduce immediately that in the SSM (i.e.
\mbox{$\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$}=\mbox{$\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$}
), the branching ratio into ordinary quarks is 3 times larger the branching ratio into leptons (i.e.
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{u_{i}d_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}=3\,\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{l_{i}l_{\nu_{i}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
). Then eq. (
61
) becomes:

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}+12\,\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{l_{i}l_{\nu_{i}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=1

(64)

Now, if we assume that
W^{\prime}
decay democratically to all kind of quarks (i.e.
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}=\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{u_{i}d_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}
), we get:

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}=\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{u_{i}d_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}=3/15

\displaystyle\equiv 20\%

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{l_{i}l_{\nu_{i}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}=1/15

\displaystyle\equiv 6.67\%.

(65)

In the CSSM (i.e.
\mbox{$\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$}=\mbox{$\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}$}=1
), the parameter
\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}
given by:

\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{u_{i}d_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{W^{\prime}}}}}\approx 2.236

(66)

which fulfill the perturbative unitarity requirement, see subsection
2.3
.

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}
for different scenario that we will consider in this work:

Table 10:
Parameters controlling the couplings.

The parameter
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}

The partial width of the different modes of the decay of
{Z^{\prime}}
are given by (all the ordinary quarks masses are neglected even the top quark):

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mbox{${Z^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow q\bar{q})=N_{\scriptscriptstyle{C}}(\mbox{$\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}$})^{2}\frac{g^{2}m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}{12\pi c_{w}^{2}}\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle qq}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mbox{${Z^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow l^{-}l^{+})=(\mbox{$\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}$})^{2}\frac{g^{2}m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}{12\pi c_{w}^{2}}\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle ll}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mbox{${Z^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow\nu_{l}\bar{\nu}_{l})=(\mbox{$\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}$})^{2}\frac{g^{2}m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}{12\pi c_{w}^{2}}\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle\nu_{l}\nu_{l}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mbox{${Z^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow Q\bar{q}+\bar{Q}q)=N_{\scriptscriptstyle{C}}(\mbox{$\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}$})^{2}\frac{g^{2}m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}{12\pi c_{w}^{2}}\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle Qq}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}

(67)

with

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle ff}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}=\sqrt{1-4\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{f}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}^{2}}}\biggl[(g_{\scriptscriptstyle{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle f})^{2}\biggl(1-4\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{f}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}^{2}}\biggr)+(g_{\scriptscriptstyle{V}}^{\scriptscriptstyle f})^{2}\biggl(1+2\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{f}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}^{2}}\biggr)\biggr]\approx[(g_{\scriptscriptstyle A}^{\scriptscriptstyle f})^{2}+(g_{\scriptscriptstyle V}^{\scriptscriptstyle f})^{2}]

(68)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle Qq}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{4}\sqrt{\lambda\biggl(1,\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}^{2}},\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}^{2}}\biggr)}\biggl[1-\frac{1}{2}\biggl(\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{2}-m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}^{2}}\biggr)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Q}}^{2}+m_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}^{2}}{m_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}^{2}}\biggr]

(69)

The total decay width of the
{Z^{\prime}}
is then given by:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}^{\text{tot}}

\displaystyle=3\,\Gamma(\mbox{${Z^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow u\bar{u})+3\,\Gamma(\mbox{${Z^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow d\bar{d})+3\,\Gamma(\mbox{${Z^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow l^{-}l^{+})+3\,\Gamma(\mbox{${Z^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow\nu_{l}\bar{\nu}_{l})+\Gamma(\mbox{${Z^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow Q\bar{q}+\bar{Q}q)

(70)

The branching fraction of
{Z^{\prime}}
to
Q\bar{q}
(or
\bar{Q}q
) in the SSM is expressed as:

\displaystyle\text{Br}(\mbox{${Z^{\prime}}$}\rightarrow Q\bar{q}+\bar{Q}q)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\mbox{$\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}$})^{2}\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}}{\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}$}^{2}}\equiv\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}.

(71)

with

\displaystyle\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}$}^{2}

\displaystyle=\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{f=l,\nu}}{\sum}(\mbox{$\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}$})^{2}[(g_{\scriptscriptstyle A}^{\scriptscriptstyle f})^{2}+(g_{\scriptscriptstyle V}^{\scriptscriptstyle f})^{2}]+\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{q=u,d}}{\sum}G_{\scriptscriptstyle{q}}(\mbox{$\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}$})^{2}[(g_{\scriptscriptstyle A}^{\scriptscriptstyle q})^{2}+(g_{\scriptscriptstyle V}^{\scriptscriptstyle q})^{2}]+\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{tt}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}+(\mbox{$\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}$})^{2}\,\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}

(72)

with
G_{\scriptscriptstyle{d}}=3
and
G_{\scriptscriptstyle{u}}=3
.

We can now write the coupling
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}
in terms of the branching ratio
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}
, the kinematic function
\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}
, and the new parameter
\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}
. Thus, we obtain:

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}$}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}}

(73)

Similar to
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(W^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}
, to avoid encountering threshold singularity in the expression of
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}
, we can slightly modify this parameterization by replacing the two key elements,
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}
and
\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}
, with their values at
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle W^{\prime}}\rightarrow 0
. This is allowed if the following specific condition is satisfied:

\displaystyle\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}=\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}0}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}0}}}\equiv\text{constant}

(74)

So, the coupling in the modified parameterization is given by:

\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}$}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}0}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}0}}}

with

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}0}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}.

(75)

In the right panel of figure
19
, we show the variation of the coupling
\kappa^{\scriptscriptstyle{(Z^{\prime})}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}
in the original parameterization (cf. eq. (
73
)) and the modified parameterization (cf. eq.

) as a function of the ratio
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}
for
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}=4
TeV. The green, red and blue curves represent, respectively, the coupling for
\mbox{$\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}$}=\mbox{$\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}$}=2,1,0.5
. We observe that the couplings in the two parameterizations are approximately the same for
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}<0.6
especially for
\mbox{$\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}$}=\mbox{$\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}$}=0.5
. This means that the two options are equivalent in this regions
25
25

25

Anyway, we showed in section 3 that the effects of the ratios
m_{\scriptscriptstyle Q}/m_{\scriptscriptstyle B^{\prime}}
on the cross sections is limted.
.

In the same manner, we can express the couplings of the
{Z^{\prime}}
to ordinary quarks and leptons in terms of the associated branchings ratios and kinematic functions, as follows:

\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}$}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{u_{i}u_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle u_{i}u_{i}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}}}=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}$}\sqrt{\frac{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{d_{i}d_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle d_{i}d_{i}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}}}

\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}$}\sqrt{\frac{3\,\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{l_{i}l_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle l_{i}l_{i}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}}}=\mbox{$\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}$}\sqrt{\frac{3\,\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{l_{\nu_{i}}l_{\nu_{i}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle l_{\nu_{i}}l_{\nu_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}}}

(76)

where the branching ratios must satisfy:

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\left[\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{u_{i}u_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}+\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{d_{i}d_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}+\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{l_{i}l_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}+\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{l_{\nu_{i}}l_{\nu_{i}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}\right]

\displaystyle=1.

(77)

It is evident from eq. (
76
), that the parameter
\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}
is not free, but it depends on the SSM parameters (
\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}
or
\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}
) the branching ratios and the kinematic functions:

\kappa_{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}$}\left[\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle u_{i}u_{i}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}/\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{u_{i}u_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}\right]^{1/2}=\mbox{$\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}$}\left[\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle l_{i}l_{i}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}/\left(3\,\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{l_{\nu_{i}}l_{\nu_{i}}}}\right)\right]^{1/2}

(78)

If we assume that
\mbox{$\varkappa_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}$}=\mbox{$\vartheta_{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}$}
(SSM), we find the following relations between the branching ratios and the kinematic functions of the ordinary fermions:

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{l_{\nu_{i}}l_{\nu_{i}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{l_{i}l_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle l_{\nu_{i}}l_{\nu_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle l_{i}l_{i}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{u_{i}u_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=3\,\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{l_{i}l_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle u_{i}u_{i}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle l_{i}l_{i}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{d_{i}d_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=3\,\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{l_{i}l_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}\,\frac{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle d_{i}d_{i}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}}{\Gamma_{\scriptscriptstyle l_{i}l_{i}}^{\scriptscriptstyle Z^{\prime}}}

(79)

Now, if we assume that
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}=\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{u_{i}u_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}},2\,\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{u_{i}u_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}},3\,\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{u_{i}u_{i}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}
we get, respectively,
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle{Qq}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{Z^{\prime}}}=9.60\%,17.50\%,24.12\%
.

Now, let’s find the parameter that describes the mixing between
Q
and
{H^{\prime}}
in the case where

. The partial decay widths of all possible decay channels of the new heavy scalar are
